People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. More like about the past month.\n\nIt seemed to start after my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. And at home… my husband and I have been arguing more than we should. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. More like about the past month.\n\nIt seemed to start after my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s when I really started noticing the focus and restlessness being harder to manage. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work or significantly cut back on your social activities due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really days off, no… I’ve still been going in and trying to keep things normal. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut it has affected things. At work I’ve had those inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which feels kind of embarrassing. And socially… I guess I haven’t cut back a lot exactly, but at home my husband and I have been arguing enough that it feels like we’re sort of walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships due to your symptoms?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still managing day to day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together. I guess that counts as affecting things, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to enjoy activities you used to find pleasurable due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, if I’m distracted or fidgety, it can be harder to settle into something, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things.\n\nMaybe I get a little frustrated with myself, but I’m still trying to do my usual things. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. And at home… my husband and I have been arguing more than we should. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. More like about the past month.\n\nIt seemed to start after my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s when I really started noticing the focus and restlessness being harder to manage. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work or significantly cut back on your social activities due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really days off, no… I’ve still been going in and trying to keep things normal. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut it has affected things. At work I’ve had those inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which feels kind of embarrassing. And socially… I guess I haven’t cut back a lot exactly, but at home my husband and I have been arguing enough that it feels like we’re sort of walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships due to your symptoms?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still managing day to day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But at work I’ve made some inventory errors, and I’ve had to go back and double-check with my supervisor, which is embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual. It feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together. I guess that counts as affecting things, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to enjoy activities you used to find pleasurable due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. It’s more like… about a month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. More like about the past month.\n\nIt seemed to start after my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s when I really started noticing the focus and restlessness being harder to manage. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work or significantly cut back on your social activities due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really days off, no… I’ve still been going in and trying to keep things normal. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut it has affected things. At work I’ve had those inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which feels kind of embarrassing. And socially… I guess I haven’t cut back a lot exactly, but at home my husband and I have been arguing enough that it feels like we’re sort of walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships due to your symptoms?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still managing day to day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But at work I’ve made some inventory errors, and I’ve had to go back and double-check with my supervisor, which is embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual. It feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together. I guess that counts as affecting things, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to enjoy activities you used to find pleasurable due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, if I’m distracted or fidgety, it can be harder to settle into something, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things.\n\nMaybe I get a little frustrated with myself, but I’m still trying to do my usual things. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to overstate it. At work I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which is kind of embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual. It’s gotten to where it feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it has affected both, at least somewhat.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for at least six months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s been that long. It’s more like… about a month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. It kind of started around then, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess… I’m having a harder time keeping my mind on one thing than I used to. It’s probably not a huge thing, but at the warehouse, little stuff like a beeper going off, or the forklift alarm, or someone moving a pallet—my attention just jumps to that. Then I lose my place on the count and I’ll realize I put the wrong numbers into inventory. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s been happening enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these difficulties with keeping your mind on one thing?\n\nPatient: Maybe about a month or so? It started around when my husband stopped working outside the home, and our regular routine kind of changed. I thought I’d adjust to it, and it’s not like I can’t function, but that’s about when I noticed it happening more.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I’m not trying to be disruptive or anything, but after my shifts I used to come home wiped out and just sit. Lately I get home and I feel kind of… buzzing, I guess. Like sitting on the couch feels almost impossible, so I’ll start reorganizing cabinets or sorting bins in the garage. It’s productive, I suppose, but it does feel like I can’t quite settle down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting on impulse more often, like doing things without thinking them through?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m doing anything wild, but… I have spent more than I probably should have at craft markets. Paints, frames, tools—things for projects I was sure would turn into something useful. At the time it felt sensible, like I had a plan for all of it, but now when I look at the credit card bill I feel pretty embarrassed.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble getting your work done at the warehouse because of these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: A little, but I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not doing my job. I’m still showing up and getting things done. I have had to double-check with my supervisor a couple times after I made some inventory errors, which is embarrassing. And at home, honestly, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual… enough that it feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement the way we should be.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel the need to be constantly active or feel uncomfortable staying in one place for too long?\n\nPatient: Yes… I guess I do, more than usual. I don’t want to overstate it, but after work I used to be tired enough to just sit down and watch TV. Lately I come home feeling kind of keyed up, like I need to be doing something. If I try to sit on the couch, it feels uncomfortable, so I end up reorganizing cabinets or sorting things out in the garage. I tell myself it’s useful, but it does feel like I can’t stay still very well.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how you interact with your friends or family members since you started experiencing these difficulties?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not terribly, I don’t think, but there have been some changes. At work I’ve had to check back in with my supervisor after making a couple inventory mistakes, which makes me feel pretty foolish.\n\nAnd at home, with my husband… we’ve been arguing more than usual. I don’t want to make it sound like things are falling apart, but it’s enough that we’re kind of walking around each other instead of just enjoying this time with him being retired. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these difficulties with focus and restlessness are affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it is, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, because I’m still working and managing things, but it’s been affecting me more than I’d like. I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor after making some inventory errors, which feels embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing enough that it kind of feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. So… yes, I guess it is affecting my quality of life some.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant mood swings or irritability lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make too much of it, but some days I feel almost too upbeat, like I can get the whole house fixed up and straighten out everything at work before dinner.\n\nAnd then if my husband asks a reasonable question about one of my plans, I’ll snap at him over something small. I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really like me. Does that count as mood swings, or is that just stress?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings, even when you're trying to focus on a task?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, and it’s honestly one of the main reasons I came in. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m careless, because I really do try, but at the warehouse if a beeper goes off, or a forklift alarm, or even someone moving a pallet nearby, my mind just jumps to that.\n\nThen I’ll go back to what I was counting and realize I’ve put the wrong numbers into inventory. It’s embarrassing. I keep thinking I should be able to tune it out better, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself so focused on a task that you tune out everything else around you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that happens, no. I mean, I can get involved in something if I’m really trying, but I don’t think I tune everything out in that way.\n\nIf anything, I tend to notice too much around me. But maybe I’m misunderstanding what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus, restlessness, and impulsivity been causing significant problems in your relationship with your husband or at work?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I hate saying it that way because I don’t want it to sound like I’m falling apart.\n\nAt work, I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to go back and double-check things with my supervisor, which is embarrassing. And at home… my husband and I have been arguing more than we should. It’s like we’re walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if these difficulties with focus and restlessness have been consistent over the past month, or have they been coming and going?\n\nPatient: It’s been fairly consistent, I think… not years or anything like that. More like about the past month.\n\nIt seemed to start after my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. I don’t want to blame that, exactly, but that’s when I really started noticing the focus and restlessness being harder to manage. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work or significantly cut back on your social activities due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Not really days off, no… I’ve still been going in and trying to keep things normal. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is.\n\nBut it has affected things. At work I’ve had those inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which feels kind of embarrassing. And socially… I guess I haven’t cut back a lot exactly, but at home my husband and I have been arguing enough that it feels like we’re sort of walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant difficulties in your ability to perform daily tasks or maintain relationships due to your symptoms?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I’m still managing day to day, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But at work I’ve made some inventory errors, and I’ve had to go back and double-check with my supervisor, which is embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual. It feels like we’re kind of walking around each other instead of settling into his retirement together. I guess that counts as affecting things, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to enjoy activities you used to find pleasurable due to these difficulties with focus and restlessness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, if I’m distracted or fidgety, it can be harder to settle into something, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to enjoy things.\n\nMaybe I get a little frustrated with myself, but I’m still trying to do my usual things. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like your difficulties with focus and restlessness have significantly impacted your ability to function at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it has, though I don’t want to overstate it. At work I’ve made some inventory mistakes, and I’ve had to double-check with my supervisor afterward, which is kind of embarrassing.\n\nAnd at home, my husband and I have been arguing more than usual. It’s gotten to where it feels like we’re walking around each other instead of just settling into his retirement together. So… yes, I guess it has affected both, at least somewhat.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for at least six months?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s been that long. It’s more like… about a month or so, since my husband stopped working outside the home and our normal routine changed. It kind of started around then, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been present for at least six months or is it still less than that?\n\nPatient: It’s still less than that, yes.
